=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a game dungeon comprised of n x n rooms arranged in a grid.
You are given a 2D array fruits of size n x n, where fruits[i][j] represents the number of fruits in the room (i, j). Three children will play in the game dungeon, with initial positions at the corner rooms (0, 0), (0, n - 1), and (n - 1, 0).
The children will make exactly n - 1 moves according to the following rules to reach the room (n - 1, n - 1):

The child starting from (0, 0) must move from their current room (i, j) to one of the rooms (i + 1, j + 1), (i + 1, j), and (i, j + 1) if the target room exists.
The child starting from (0, n - 1) must move from their current room (i, j) to one of the rooms (i + 1, j - 1), (i + 1, j), and (i + 1, j + 1) if the target room exists.
The child starting from (n - 1, 0) must move from their current room (i, j) to one of the rooms (i - 1, j + 1), (i, j + 1), and (i + 1, j + 1) if the target room exists.

When a child enters a room, they will collect all the fruits there. If two or more children enter the same room, only one child will collect the fruits, and the room will be emptied after they leave.
Return the maximum number of fruits the children can collect from the dungeon.
 
Example 1:

Input: fruits = [[1,2,3,4],[5,6,8,7],[9,10,11,12],[13,14,15,16]]
Output: 100
Explanation:

In this example:

The 1^st child (green) moves on the path (0,0) -> (1,1) -> (2,2) -> (3, 3).
The 2^nd child (red) moves on the path (0,3) -> (1,2) -> (2,3) -> (3, 3).
The 3^rd child (blue) moves on the path (3,0) -> (3,1) -> (3,2) -> (3, 3).

In total they collect 1 + 6 + 11 + 16 + 4 + 8 + 12 + 13 + 14 + 15 = 100 fruits.

Example 2:

Input: fruits = [[1,1],[1,1]]
Output: 4
Explanation:
In this example:

The 1^st child moves on the path (0,0) -> (1,1).
The 2^nd child moves on the path (0,1) -> (1,1).
The 3^rd child moves on the path (1,0) -> (1,1).

In total they collect 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 = 4 fruits.

 
Constraints:

2 <= n == fruits.length == fruits[i].length <= 1000
0 <= fruits[i][j] <= 1000

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxCollectedFruits(self, fruits: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, final code:
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxCollectedFruits(self, fruits: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(fruits)
        if n == 1:
            return fruits[0][0]
        ans = sum(fruits[i][i] for i in range(n))
        NEG = -10**18

        # second child
        dp = [NEG] * n
        dp[n-1] = fruits[0][n-1]  # start, not diagonal for n>1
        for i in range(1, n):
            ndp = [NEG] * n
            # valid columns j >= i
            for j in range(i, n):
                best = dp[j]  # from (i-1, j)
                if j > 0:
                    best = max(best, dp[j-1])
                if j + 1 < n:
                    best = max(best, dp[j+1])
                if best == NEG:
                    continue
                val = 0 if i == j else fruits[i][j]
                ndp[j] = best + val
            dp = ndp
        ans += dp[n-1]

        # third child
        dp = [NEG] * n
        dp[n-1] = fruits[n-1][0]  # start
        for j in range(1, n):
            ndp = [NEG] * n
            # valid rows r >= j
            for r in range(j, n):
                best = dp[r]
                if r > 0:
                    best = max(best, dp[r-1])
                if r + 1 < n:
                    best = max(best, dp[r+1])
                if best == NEG:
                    continue
                val = 0 if r == j else fruits[r][j]
                ndp[r] = best + val
            dp = ndp
        ans += dp[n-1]

        return ans

Need check B transition: For current j, previous column k can be j-1 (down-right? from (i-1,j-1) to (i,j) yes), j (down), j+1 (down-left). We take max dp[j-1], dp[j], dp[j+1]. In code best=dp[j]; if j>0 max dp[j-1]; if j+1<n max dp[j+1]. Good.

C transition: For current row r at column j, previous row p can be r-1 (from p=r-1 to r down-right), r (right), r+1 (up-right). We take max dp[r-1], dp[r], dp[r+1]. Code best=dp[r]; if r>0 max dp[r-1]; if r+1<n max dp[r+1]. Good.

But wait, for C, if previous row p = r-1, move from (p,j-1) to (r,j) is (i+1,j+1) with i=p, yes down-right. If p = r+1, move is (i-1,j+1) up-right. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "dp[n-1] = fruits[n-1][0]" for C. If n>1, non
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxCollectedFruits(self, fruits: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(fruits)
        if n == 1:
            return fruits[0][0]

        # The first child must move diagonally every time.
        ans = sum(fruits[i][i] for i in range(n))
        NEG = -10**18

        # Second child: from (0, n-1) to (n-1, n-1).
        # Row increases by 1 each move.
        dp = [NEG] * n
        dp[n - 1] = fruits[0][n - 1]

        for i in range(1, n):
            ndp = [NEG] * n
            for j in range(i, n):          # valid cells satisfy j >= i
                best = dp[j]
                if j > 0:
                    best = max(best, dp[j - 1])
                if j + 1 < n:
                    best = max(best, dp[j + 1])

                if best == NEG:
                    continue

                val = 0 if i == j else fruits[i][j]  # diagonal is collected by child 1
                ndp[j] = best + val

            dp = ndp

        ans += dp[n - 1]

        # Third child: from (n-1, 0) to (n-1, n-1).
        # Column increases by 1 each move.
        dp = [NEG] * n
        dp[n - 1] = fruits[n - 1][0]

        for j in range(1, n):
            ndp = [NEG] * n
            for r in range(j, n):          # valid cells satisfy r >= j
                best = dp[r]
                if r > 0:
                    best = max(best, dp[r - 1])
                if r + 1 < n:
                    best = max(best, dp[r + 1])

                if best == NEG:
                    continue

                val = 0 if r == j else fruits[r][j]  # diagonal is collected by child 1
                ndp[r] = best + val

            dp = ndp

        ans += dp[n - 1]

        return ans
```
